Ethnic origins in New Orleans (2020)

Growing into a predominantly Black and African-American city by race and ethnicity since 1990,[^cite_note-census1-177] in 2010 the racial and ethnic makeup of New Orleans was 60.2% Black and African American, 33.0% [White](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_Americans), 2.9% [Asian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_Americans) (1.7% Vietnamese, 0.3% Indian, 0.3% Chinese, 0.1% Filipino, 0.1% Korean), 0.0% [Pacific Islander](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Islander_American), and 1.7% people of [two or more races](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multiracial_Americans).[^cite_note-\:3-188] People of [Hispanic or Latino American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hispanic_and_Latino_Americans) origin made up 5.3% of the population; 1.3% were Mexican, 1.3% Honduran, 0.4% Cuban, 0.3% Puerto Rican, and 0.3% Nicaraguan.

In 2020, the racial and ethnic makeup of the city was 53.61% Black or African American, 31.61% [non-Hispanic white](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-Hispanic_white), 0.2% [American Indian and Alaska Native](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Native_Americans_in_the_United_States), 0.03% Pacific Islander, 3.71% multiracial or of another race, and 8.08% Hispanic and Latino American of any race.[^cite_note-\:5-175] The growth of the Hispanic and Latino population in New Orleans proper from 2010 to 2020 reflected national demographic trends of diversification throughout regions once predominantly non-Hispanic white.[^cite_note-189] Additionally, the 2020 census revealed the city now has a more diverse population than it did before Katrina, yet 21% fewer people than it had in 2000.[^cite_note-190] Out of [Louisiana's 64 parishes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_parishes_in_Louisiana), it is [one of six that have an African-American majority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._communities_with_African-American_majority_populations_in_2020#Counties) as of the 2020 census.

As of 2011, the Hispanic and Latino American population had also grown in the Greater New Orleans area alongside Black and African American residents, including in [Kenner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kenner,_Louisiana), central [Metairie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metairie,_Louisiana), and [Terrytown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrytown,_Louisiana) in Jefferson Parish and Eastern New Orleans and Mid-City in New Orleans proper.[^cite_note-HispboomKENNER-191] [Janet Murguía](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Janet_Murgu%C3%ADa), president and chief executive officer of the [UnidosUS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UnidosUS), stated that up to 120,000 Hispanic and Latino Americans workers lived in New Orleans. In June 2007, one study stated that the Hispanic and Latino American population had risen from 15,000, pre-Katrina, to over 50,000.[^cite_note-192]

After Katrina the small [Brazilian American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Americans) population expanded. Portuguese speakers were the second most numerous group to take [English as a second language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_as_a_second_language) classes in the Roman Catholic Archdiocese of New Orleans, after Spanish speakers. Many Brazilians worked in skilled trades such as tile and flooring, although fewer worked as day laborers than other Hispanic and Latino Americans. Many had moved from Brazilian communities in the [northeastern United States](431669.md), and Florida and Georgia. Brazilians settled throughout the metropolitan area; most were undocumented. In January 2008, the New Orleans Brazilian population had a mid-range estimate of 3,000 people. By 2008, Brazilians had opened many small churches, shops and restaurants catering to their community.[^cite_note-193]

Among the growing [Asian American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_Americans) community, the earliest [Filipino Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filipino_Americans) to live within the city arrived in the early 1800s.[^cite_note-194] The Vietnamese American community grew to become the largest by 2010 as many fled the aftermath of the [Vietnam War](32611.md) in the 1970s.[^cite_note-195]

### Sexual orientation and gender identity

See also: [LGBTQ rights in Louisiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBTQ_rights_in_Louisiana)



2016 New Orleans Pride

New Orleans and its metropolitan area have historically been popular destinations for [lesbian, gay, bisexual, and transgender](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT) communities.[^cite_note-196][^cite_note-197] In 2015, a [Gallup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gallup_\(company\)) survey determined New Orleans was one of the largest cities in the American South with a significant LGBT population.[^cite_note-198][^cite_note-199] Much of the LGBT community in New Orleans lives near the Central Business District, Mid-City, and Uptown; several gay bars and nightclubs are present in those areas.[^cite_note-200]

### Religion



[Cathedral-Basilica of St. Louis, King of France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Louis_Cathedral,_New_Orleans)



Beth Israel synagogue building on Carondelet Street

New Orleans's colonial history of French and Spanish settlement generated a strong [Roman Catholic](606848.md) tradition. Catholic missions ministered to slaves and free people of color and established schools for them. In addition, many late 19th and early 20th century European immigrants, such as the Irish, some Germans, and Italians were Catholic. Within the [Roman Catholic Archdiocese of New Orleans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_New_Orleans) (which includes not only the city but the surrounding parishes as well), 40% percent of the population was Roman Catholic since 2016.[^cite_note-201] Catholicism is reflected in French and Spanish cultural traditions, including its many [parochial schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parochial_schools), street names, architecture and festivals, including [Mardi Gras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Orleans_Mardi_Gras). Within the city and metropolitan area, [Catholicism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Catholicism) is also reflected in the Black and African cultural traditions with Gospel mass.[^cite_note-202] The statue of [Our Lady of Prompt Succor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Our_Lady_of_Prompt_Succor) is a notable symbol of the Catholic faith in New Orleans and throughout Louisiana.

Influenced by the [Bible Belt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bible_Belt)'s prominent [Protestant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestantism) population, New Orleans also has a sizable non-Catholic Christian demographic. Roughly the majority of Protestant Christians were [Baptist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baptists), and the city proper's largest non-Catholic bodies were the [Southern Baptist Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Baptist_Convention), the [National Missionary Baptist Convention of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Missionary_Baptist_Convention_of_America), [non-denominationals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nondenominational_Christianity), the [National Baptist Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Baptist_Convention,_USA,_Inc.), the [United Methodist Church](32304.md), the [Episcopal Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Episcopal_Church_\(United_States\)), the [African Methodist Episcopal Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_Methodist_Episcopal_Church), the [National Baptist Convention of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Baptist_Convention_of_America_International,_Inc.), and the [Church of God in Christ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_God_in_Christ) according to the [Association of Religion Data Archives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Association_of_Religion_Data_Archives) in 2020.[^cite_note-\:22-203]

New Orleans displays a distinctive variety of [Louisiana Voodoo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louisiana_Voodoo), due in part to [syncretism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syncretism) with African and Afro-Caribbean Roman Catholic beliefs. The fame of voodoo practitioner [Marie Laveau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marie_Laveau) contributed to this, as did New Orleans's Caribbean cultural influences.[^cite_note-204][^cite_note-205][^cite_note-BostonGlobeNorthernmost-206] Although the tourism industry strongly associated Voodoo with the city, only a small number of people are serious adherents.



Popp Fountain in City Park, a meeting place for The Religious Order of Witchcraft

New Orleans was also home to the occultist [Mary Oneida Toups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mary_Oneida_Toups), who was nicknamed the "Witch Queen of New Orleans". Toups' coven, The Religious Order of Witchcraft, was the first coven to be officially recognized as a religious institution by the state of Louisiana.[^cite_note-207] They would meet at Popp Fountain in [City Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_Park_\(New_Orleans\)).[^cite_note-208]

Jewish settlers, primarily [Sephardim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sephardim), settled in New Orleans from the early nineteenth century. Some migrated from the communities established in the colonial years in [Charleston, South Carolina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charleston,_South_Carolina), and [Savannah, Georgia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Savannah,_Georgia). The merchant [Abraham Cohen Labatt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abraham_Cohen_Labatt) helped found the first Jewish congregation in New Orleans in the 1830s, which became known as the [Portuguese Jewish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_and_Portuguese_Jews) Nefutzot Yehudah congregation (he and some other members were [Sephardic Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sephardic_Jews), whose ancestors had lived in Portugal and Spain). [Ashkenazi Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashkenazi_Jews) from eastern Europe immigrated in the late 19th and 20th centuries.

By the beginning of the 21st century, 10,000 [Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jew) lived in New Orleans. This number dropped to 7,000 after Hurricane Katrina, but rose again after efforts to incentivize the community's growth resulted in the arrival of about an additional 2,000 Jews.[^cite_note-209] New Orleans synagogues lost members, but most re-opened in their original locations. The exception was [Congregation Beth Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congregation_Beth_Israel_\(New_Orleans\)), the oldest and most prominent [Orthodox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orthodox_Judaism) synagogue in the New Orleans region. Beth Israel's building in Lakeview was destroyed by flooding. After seven years of holding services in temporary quarters, the congregation consecrated a new synagogue on land purchased from the [Reform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reform_Judaism) Congregation Gates of Prayer in [Metairie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metairie,_Louisiana).[^cite_note-Beth_Israel_7_years-210]

A visible religious minority,[^cite_note-211][^cite_note-212] [Muslims](19541.md) constituted 0.6% of the religious population as of 2019 according to [Sperling's BestPlaces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sperling's_BestPlaces).[^cite_note-\:4-213] The Association of Religion Data Archives in 2020 estimated that there were 6,150 Muslims in the city proper. The Islamic demographic in New Orleans and its metropolitan area have been mainly made up of Middle Eastern immigrants and [African Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African-American_Muslims).

## Economy



A [tanker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tank_ship) on the Mississippi River in New Orleans



[Intracoastal Waterway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intracoastal_Waterway) near New Orleans

New Orleans operates [one of the world's largest and busiest ports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_New_Orleans) and metropolitan New Orleans is a center of maritime industry.[^cite_note-214] The region accounts for a significant portion of the nation's [oil refining](195137.md) and [petrochemical production](67728.md), and serves as a [white-collar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White-collar_worker) corporate base for onshore and offshore [petroleum](8082027.md) and [natural gas](22131.md) production. Since the beginning of the 21st century, New Orleans has also grown into a technology hub.[^cite_note-215][^cite_note-216]

New Orleans is also a center for [higher learning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Higher_learning), with over 50,000 students enrolled in the region's eleven two- and four-year degree-granting institutions. [Tulane University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tulane_University), a top-50 research university, is located in Uptown. Metropolitan New Orleans is a major regional hub for the [health care industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_care_industry) and boasts a small, globally competitive manufacturing sector. The center city possesses a rapidly growing, entrepreneurial [creative industries](674483.md) sector and is renowned for its [cultural tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_tourism). Greater New Orleans, Inc. (GNO, Inc.)[^cite_note-217] acts as the first point-of-contact for regional economic development, coordinating between Louisiana's Department of Economic Development and the various business development agencies.

### Port

New Orleans began as a strategically located trading *[entrepôt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entrep%C3%B4t)* and it remains, above all, a crucial transportation hub and distribution center for waterborne commerce. The [Port of New Orleans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_New_Orleans) is the fifth-largest in the United States based on cargo volume, and second-largest in the state after the [Port of South Louisiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_South_Louisiana). It is the twelfth-largest in the U.S. based on cargo value. The Port of South Louisiana, also located in the New Orleans area, is the world's busiest in terms of bulk tonnage. When combined with Port of New Orleans, it forms the 4th-largest port system in volume. Many shipbuilding, shipping, logistics, freight forwarding and commodity brokerage firms either are based in metropolitan New Orleans or maintain a local presence. Examples include [Intermarine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intermarine),[^cite_note-218] Bisso Towboat,[^cite_note-219] [Northrop Grumman Ship Systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northrop_Grumman_Ship_Systems),[^cite_note-220] Trinity Yachts, [Expeditors International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expeditors_International),[^cite_note-221] Bollinger Shipyards, IMTT, International Coffee Corp, Boasso America, Transoceanic Shipping, Transportation Consultants Inc., Dupuy Storage & Forwarding and Silocaf.[^cite_note-222] The largest coffee-roasting plant in the world, operated by [Folgers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folgers), is located in [New Orleans East](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_New_Orleans).[^cite_note-223][^cite_note-224]



The steamboat [*Natchez*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natchez_\(boat\)) operates out of New Orleans.

New Orleans is located near to the [Gulf of Mexico](21076367.md) and its many oil rigs. Louisiana ranks fifth among states in oil production and eighth in [reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_reserves). It has two of the four [Strategic Petroleum Reserve](167198.md) (SPR) storage facilities: West Hackberry in [Cameron Parish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cameron_Parish,_Louisiana) and Bayou Choctaw in [Iberville Parish](97696.md). The area hosts 17 petroleum refineries, with a combined crude oil distillation capacity of nearly 2.8 million barrels per day (450,000 m<sup>3</sup>/d), the second highest after Texas. Louisiana's numerous ports include the [Louisiana Offshore Oil Port](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louisiana_Offshore_Oil_Port) (LOOP), which is capable of receiving the largest oil tankers. Given the quantity of oil imports, Louisiana is home to many major pipelines: [Crude Oil](23195.md) ([Exxon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exxon), [Chevron](284749.md), [BP](18998720.md), [Texaco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texaco), [Shell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shell_Oil_Company), Scurloch-Permian, Mid-Valley, Calumet, [Conoco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conoco_Inc.), [Koch Inc.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koch_Inc.?action=edit\&redlink=1), [Unocal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unocal_Corporation), [U.S. Dept. of Energy](62866.md), Locap); Product ([TEPPCO Partners](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TEPPCO_Partners), Colonial, Plantation, Explorer, Texaco, Collins); and [Liquefied Petroleum Gas](361384.md) (Dixie, TEPPCO, Black Lake, Koch, Chevron, [Dynegy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dynegy), [Kinder Morgan Energy Partners](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kinder_Morgan_Energy_Partners), [Dow Chemical Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dow_Chemical_Company), Bridgeline, FMP, Tejas, Texaco, UTP).[^cite_note-225] Several energy companies have regional headquarters in the area, including [Shell plc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shell_plc), [Eni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eni) and Chevron. Other energy producers and [oilfield services companies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oilfield_services) are headquartered in the city or region, and the sector supports a large professional services base of specialized engineering and design firms, as well as a term office for the federal government's [Minerals Management Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minerals_Management_Service).

### Business

The city is the home to a single [Fortune 500](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortune_500) company: [Entergy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entergy), a power generation utility and [nuclear power plant](207347.md) operations specialist.[^cite_note-226] After Katrina, the city lost its other Fortune 500 company, [Freeport-McMoRan](588316.md), when it merged its copper and gold exploration unit with an Arizona company and relocated that division to [Phoenix](49121.md). Its McMoRan Exploration affiliate remains headquartered in New Orleans.[^cite_note-227]

Companies with significant operations or headquarters in New Orleans include: Pan American Life Insurance, Pool Corp, [Rolls-Royce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rolls-Royce_plc), Newpark Resources, [AT\&T](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AT\&T_Inc.), TurboSquid, iSeatz, [IBM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IBM), Navtech, [Superior Energy Services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superior_Energy_Services), [Textron Marine & Land Systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Textron_Marine_&_Land_Systems), [McDermott International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McDermott_International), Pellerin Milnor, [Lockheed Martin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lockheed_Martin), Imperial Trading, Laitram, [Harrah's Entertainment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harrah's_Entertainment), Stewart Enterprises, [Edison Chouest Offshore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edison_Chouest_Offshore), [Zatarain's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zatarain's), Waldemar S. Nelson & Co., [Whitney National Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whitney_National_Bank), [Capital One](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capital_One), [Tidewater Marine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tidewater_\(marine_services\)), [Popeyes Chicken & Biscuits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popeyes_Chicken_&_Biscuits), [Parsons Brinckerhoff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parsons_Brinckerhoff), [MWH Global](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MWH_Global), [CH2M Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CH2M_Hill), Energy Partners Ltd, The Receivables Exchange, [GE Capital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GE_Capital), and [Smoothie King](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smoothie_King).

Entrepreneurship is encouraged, with a number of initiatives designed to support small business and start-ups, including [Entrepreneurs' Row](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entrepreneurs'_Row) and the New Orleans Startup Fund.[^cite_note-228]

### Tourist and convention business

Tourism is a staple of the city's economy. Perhaps more visible than any other sector, New Orleans's tourist and convention industry is a \$5.5 billion industry that accounts for 40 percent of city tax revenues. In 2004, the hospitality industry employed 85,000 people, making it the city's top economic sector as measured by employment.[^cite_note-0607marketing-229] New Orleans also hosts the World Cultural Economic Forum (WCEF). The forum, held annually at the [New Orleans Morial Convention Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Orleans_Morial_Convention_Center), is directed toward promoting cultural and economic development opportunities through the strategic convening of cultural ambassadors and leaders from around the world. The first WCEF took place in October 2008.[^cite_note-230]

### Federal and military agencies



Aerial view of NASA's Michoud Assembly Facility

Federal agencies and the Armed forces operate significant facilities there. The [U.S. Fifth Circuit Court of Appeals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Court_of_Appeals_for_the_Fifth_Circuit) operates at the US. Courthouse downtown. [NASA](18426568.md)'s [Michoud Assembly Facility](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michoud_Assembly_Facility) is located in [New Orleans East](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_New_Orleans) and has multiple tenants including [Lockheed Martin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lockheed_Martin) and [Boeing](18933266.md). It is a huge manufacturing complex that produced the external fuel tanks for the [Space Shuttles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_Shuttle), the [Saturn V](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saturn_V) first stage, the [Integrated Truss Structure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Integrated_Truss_Structure) of the [International Space Station](15043.md), and is now used for the construction of NASA's [Space Launch System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_Launch_System). The rocket factory lies within the enormous New Orleans Regional Business Park, also home to the [National Finance Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Finance_Center), operated by the [United States Department of Agriculture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Department_of_Agriculture) (USDA), and the Crescent Crown distribution center. Other large governmental installations include the U.S. Navy's [Space and Naval Warfare (SPAWAR) Systems Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_and_Naval_Warfare_Systems_Command), located within the University of New Orleans Research and Technology Park in [Gentilly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gentilly,_New_Orleans), [Naval Air Station Joint Reserve Base New Orleans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naval_Air_Station_Joint_Reserve_Base_New_Orleans); and the headquarters for the Marine Force Reserves in Federal City in [Algiers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algiers,_New_Orleans).

## Culture and contemporary life

Main article: [Culture of New Orleans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_New_Orleans)

### Tourism

See also: [Culture of New Orleans § Museums and other attractions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_New_Orleans#Museums_and_other_attractions)

New Orleans has many visitor attractions, from the world-renowned French Quarter to [St. Charles Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Charles_Avenue), (home of Tulane and Loyola universities, the historic [Pontchartrain Hotel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pontchartrain_Hotel) and many 19th-century mansions) to [Magazine Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magazine_Street) with its boutique stores and antique shops.



The [French Quarter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Quarter) in 2009



A [street artist](524268.md) in the French Quarter, 1988

According to current travel guides, New Orleans is one of the top ten most-visited cities in the United States; 10.1 million visitors came to New Orleans in 2004.[^cite_note-0607marketing-229][^cite_note-231] Prior to Katrina, 265 hotels with 38,338 rooms operated in the Greater New Orleans Area. In May 2007, that had declined to some 140 hotels and motels with over 31,000 rooms.[^cite_note-New_Orleans'_Recovery_as_of_May_2007-232]

A 2009 *[Travel + Leisure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Travel_+_Leisure)* poll of "America's Favorite Cities" ranked New Orleans first in ten categories, the most first-place rankings of the 30 cities included. According to the poll, New Orleans was the best U.S. city as a spring break destination and for "wild weekends", stylish boutique hotels, cocktail hours, singles/bar scenes, live music/concerts and bands, antique and vintage shops, cafés/coffee bars, neighborhood restaurants, and [people watching](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People_watching). The city ranked second for: friendliness (behind [Charleston, South Carolina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charleston,_South_Carolina)), [gay](5171128.md)-friendliness (behind San Francisco), [bed and breakfast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bed_and_breakfast) hotels/inns, and ethnic food. However, the city placed near the bottom in cleanliness, safety and as a family destination.[^cite_note-233][^cite_note-234]

The French Quarter (known locally as "the Quarter" or *Vieux Carré*), which was the colonial-era city and is bounded by the Mississippi River, Rampart Street, [Canal Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canal_Street,_New_Orleans), and [Esplanade Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Esplanade_Avenue,_New_Orleans), contains popular hotels, bars and nightclubs. Notable tourist attractions in the Quarter include Bourbon Street, Jackson Square, St. Louis Cathedral, the [French Market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Market) (including [Café du Monde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caf%C3%A9_du_Monde), famous for [café au lait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caf%C3%A9_au_lait) and [beignets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beignet)) and [Preservation Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Preservation_Hall). Also in the French Quarter is the old [New Orleans Mint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Orleans_Mint), a former branch of the [United States Mint](358818.md) which now operates as a museum, and [The Historic New Orleans Collection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Historic_New_Orleans_Collection), a museum and research center housing art and artifacts relating to the [history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_New_Orleans) and the [Gulf South](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_South).

Close to the Quarter is the [Tremé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trem%C3%A9) community, which contains the [New Orleans Jazz National Historical Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Orleans_Jazz_National_Historical_Park) and the [New Orleans African American Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Orleans_African_American_Museum)—a site which is listed on the [Louisiana African American Heritage Trail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louisiana_African_American_Heritage_Trail).

The *[Natchez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natchez_\(boat\))* is an authentic [steamboat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steamboat) with a [calliope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calliope_\(music\)) that cruises the length of the city twice daily. Unlike most other places in the United States, New Orleans has become widely known for its [elegant decay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elegant_decay). The city's [historic cemeteries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historic_Cemeteries_of_New_Orleans) and their distinct above-ground [tombs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tomb) are attractions in themselves, the oldest and most famous of which, [Saint Louis Cemetery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Louis_Cemetery), greatly resembles [Père Lachaise Cemetery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/P%C3%A8re_Lachaise_Cemetery) in Paris.



The [New Orleans Museum of Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Orleans_Museum_of_Art) (NOMA) located in [City Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_Park,_New_Orleans)

[The National WWII Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_National_WWII_Museum) offers a multi-building odyssey through the history of the Pacific and European theaters. Nearby, [Confederate Memorial Hall Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confederate_Memorial_Hall_Museum), the oldest continually operating museum in Louisiana (although under renovation since Hurricane Katrina), contains the second-largest collection of [Confederate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confederate_States_of_America) memorabilia. Art museums include the [Contemporary Arts Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contemporary_Arts_Center_\(New_Orleans\)), the [New Orleans Museum of Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Orleans_Museum_of_Art) (NOMA) in [City Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_Park_\(New_Orleans\)), and the [Ogden Museum of Southern Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ogden_Museum_of_Southern_Art).

New Orleans is home to the [Audubon Nature Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Audubon_Nature_Institute) (which consists of [Audubon Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Audubon_Park_\(New_Orleans\)), the [Audubon Zoo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Audubon_Zoo), the [Aquarium of the Americas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aquarium_of_the_Americas) and the [Audubon Insectarium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Audubon_Insectarium)), and home to gardens which include [Longue Vue House and Gardens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Longue_Vue_House_and_Gardens) and the [New Orleans Botanical Garden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Orleans_Botanical_Garden). City Park, one of the country's most expansive and visited [urban parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_park), has one of the largest stands of [oak trees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_live_oak) in the world.

Other points of interest can be found in the surrounding areas. Many wetlands are found nearby, including [Honey Island Swamp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honey_Island_Swamp) and [Barataria Preserve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean_Lafitte_National_Historical_Park_and_Preserve#Barataria_Preserve). [Chalmette Battlefield and National Cemetery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean_Lafitte_National_Historical_Park_and_Preserve#Chalmette_unit), located just south of the city, is the site of the 1815 [Battle of New Orleans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_New_Orleans).

### Entertainment and performing arts

Main article: [Music of New Orleans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_New_Orleans)



[New Orleans Mardi Gras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Orleans_Mardi_Gras) in the early 1890s



Mounted [krewe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krewe) officers in the Thoth Parade during [Mardi Gras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mardi_Gras)

The New Orleans area is home to numerous annual celebrations. The most well known is [Carnival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carnival), or [Mardi Gras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Orleans_Mardi_Gras). Carnival officially begins on the [Feast of the Epiphany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epiphany_\(holiday\)), also known in some Christian traditions as the "[Twelfth Night](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twelfth_Night_\(holiday\))" of Christmas. *Mardi Gras* (French for "Fat Tuesday"), the final and grandest day of traditional Catholic festivities, is the last Tuesday before the [Christian](5211.md) liturgical season of [Lent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lent), which commences on [Ash Wednesday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ash_Wednesday).

The largest of the city's many music festivals is the [New Orleans Jazz & Heritage Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Orleans_Jazz_&_Heritage_Festival). Commonly referred to simply as "Jazz Fest", it is one of the nation's largest music festivals. The festival features a variety of music, including both native Louisiana and international artists. Along with Jazz Fest, New Orleans's [Voodoo Experience](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voodoo_Experience) ("Voodoo Fest") and the [Essence Music Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Essence_Music_Festival) also feature local and international artists.

Other major festivals include [Southern Decadence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Decadence), the French Quarter Festival, and the [Tennessee Williams/New Orleans Literary Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennessee_Williams/New_Orleans_Literary_Festival). The American playwright lived and wrote in New Orleans early in his career, and set his play, *[Streetcar Named Desire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Streetcar_Named_Desire),* there.



[Louis Prima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_Prima); a famous New Orleans [jazz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jazz), [swing music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swing_music), and [jump blues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jump_blues), musician.&#x20;

In 2002, Louisiana began offering tax incentives for film and television production. Films produced in and around the city include *[Ray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ray_\(film\))*, *[Runaway Jury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Runaway_Jury)*, *[The Pelican Brief](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pelican_Brief_\(film\))*, *[Glory Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glory_Road_\(film\))*, *[All the King's Men](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All_the_King's_Men_\(2006_film\))*, *[Déjà Vu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deja_Vu_\(2006_film\))*, *[Last Holiday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Last_Holiday_\(2006_film\))*, *[The Curious Case of Benjamin Button](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Curious_Case_of_Benjamin_Button_\(film\))*, *[12 Years a Slave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/12_Years_a_Slave_\(film\))*, and *[Project Power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Project_Power)*.



[Louis Armstrong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_Armstrong), famous New Orleans [jazz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jazz) musician

The first theatre in New Orleans was the French-language [Theatre de la Rue Saint Pierre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theatre_de_la_Rue_Saint_Pierre), which opened in 1792. The first opera in New Orleans was performed there in 1796. In the nineteenth century, the city was the home of two of America's most important venues for [French opera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_opera), the [Théâtre d'Orléans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Th%C3%A9%C3%A2tre_d'Orl%C3%A9ans) and later the [French Opera House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Opera_House). Today, opera is performed by the [New Orleans Opera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Orleans_Opera). The [Marigny Opera House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marigny_Opera_House) is home to the Marigny Opera Ballet and also hosts opera, jazz, and classical music performances.



[Frank Ocean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frank_Ocean) is a musician from New Orleans.

New Orleans has long been a significant center for music, showcasing its intertwined European, African and Latino American cultures. The city's unique musical heritage was born in its colonial and early American days from a unique blending of European musical instruments with African rhythms. As the only North American city to have allowed [slaves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slavery_in_the_United_States) to gather in public and play their native music (largely in [Congo Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congo_Square), now located within [Louis Armstrong Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_Armstrong_Park_\(New_Orleans\))), New Orleans gave birth in the early 20th century to an epochal indigenous music: [jazz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jazz). Soon, African American [brass bands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brass_band) formed, beginning a century-long tradition. The Louis Armstrong Park area, near the French Quarter in Tremé, contains the [New Orleans Jazz National Historical Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Orleans_Jazz_National_Historical_Park). The city's music was later also significantly influenced by [Acadiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acadiana), home of [Cajun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cajun_music) and [Zydeco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zydeco) music, and by [Delta blues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blues).

New Orleans's unique musical culture is on display in its traditional funerals. A spin on military funerals, New Orleans's traditional funerals feature sad music (mostly [dirges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dirge) and [hymns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hymn)) in processions on the way to the cemetery and happier music (hot jazz) on the way back. Until the 1990s, most locals preferred to call these "funerals with music". Visitors to the city have long dubbed them "[jazz funerals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jazz_funeral)".

Much later in its musical development, New Orleans was home to a distinctive brand of [rhythm and blues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rhythm_and_blues) that contributed greatly to the growth of [rock and roll](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rock_and_roll). An example of the New Orleans's sound in the 1960s is the No. 1 U.S. hit "[Chapel of Love](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chapel_of_Love)" by [the Dixie Cups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Dixie_Cups), a song which knocked [the Beatles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Beatles) out of the top spot on the [Billboard Hot 100](423161.md). New Orleans became a hotbed for [funk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Funk) music in the 1960s and 1970s, and by the late 1980s, it had developed its own localized variant of [hip-hop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hip-hop), called [bounce music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bounce_music). While not commercially successful outside of the [Deep South](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deep_South), bounce music was immensely popular in poorer neighborhoods throughout the 1990s.

A cousin of bounce, [New Orleans hip-hop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_hip_hop) achieved commercial success locally and internationally, producing [Lil Wayne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lil_Wayne), [Master P](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Master_P), [Birdman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Birdman_\(rapper\)), [Juvenile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juvenile_\(rapper\)), [Suicideboys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicideboys), [Cash Money Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cash_Money_Records) and [No Limit Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/No_Limit_Records). Additionally, the popularity of [cowpunk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cowpunk), a fast form of [southern rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_rock), originated with the help of several local bands, such as [The Radiators](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Radiators_\(US\)), [Better Than Ezra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Better_Than_Ezra), [Cowboy Mouth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cowboy_Mouth) and [Dash Rip Rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dash_Rip_Rock). Throughout the 1990s, many [sludge metal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sludge_metal) bands started. New Orleans's [heavy metal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heavy_metal_music) bands such as [Eyehategod](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eyehategod),[^cite_note-AMG-Eyehategod-235] [Soilent Green](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soilent_Green),[^cite_note-AMG-SoilentGreen-236] [Crowbar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crowbar_\(US_band\)),[^cite_note-AMG-Crowbar-237] and [Down](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Down_\(band\)) incorporated styles such as [hardcore punk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hardcore_punk),[^cite_note-AMG-Down-238] [doom metal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doom_metal), and southern rock to create an original and heady brew of swampy and aggravated metal that has largely avoided standardization.[^cite_note-AMG-Eyehategod-235][^cite_note-AMG-SoilentGreen-236][^cite_note-AMG-Crowbar-237][^cite_note-AMG-Down-238]

New Orleans is the southern terminus of the famed [Highway 61](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Route_61), made musically famous by musician [Bob Dylan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Dylan) in his song, "[Highway 61 Revisited](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Highway_61_Revisited)".

### Cuisine



Steamship *Bienville* on-board restaurant menu (April 7, 1861)

Main articles: [Cuisine of New Orleans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuisine_of_New_Orleans), [Louisiana Creole cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louisiana_Creole_cuisine), and [Cajun cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cajun_cuisine)

New Orleans is world-famous for its cuisine. The indigenous cuisine is distinctive and influential. New Orleans food combined local Creole, haute Creole and New Orleans French cuisines. Local ingredients, French, Spanish, Italian, African, Native American, Cajun, Chinese, and a hint of Cuban traditions combine to produce a truly unique and easily recognizable New Orleans flavor.

New Orleans is known for specialties including [beignets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beignet) (locally pronounced like "ben-yays"), square-shaped fried dough that could be called "French doughnuts" (served with *[café au lait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caf%C3%A9_au_lait)* made with a blend of coffee and chicory rather than only coffee); and [po' boy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Po'_boy)[^cite_note-239] and Italian [muffuletta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muffuletta) sandwiches; Gulf oysters on the half-shell, fried oysters, boiled [crawfish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crayfish) and other [seafood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seafood); [étouffée](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89touff%C3%A9e), [jambalaya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jambalaya), [gumbo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gumbo) and other Creole dishes; and the Monday favorite of [red beans and rice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_beans_and_rice) ([Louis Armstrong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_Armstrong) often signed his letters, "Red beans and ricely yours"). Another New Orleans specialty is the [praline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pralin) locally [/ˈprɑːliːn/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/English), a candy made with brown sugar, granulated sugar, cream, butter, and pecans. The city offers notable street food[^cite_note-240] including the Asian inspired beef [Yaka mein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yaka_mein).

### Dialect

Main article: [New Orleans English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Orleans_English)

See also: [Culture of New Orleans § Language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_New_Orleans#Language)



[Café du Monde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caf%C3%A9_du_Monde), a landmark New Orleans beignet cafe established in 1862

New Orleans developed a distinctive local dialect that is neither [Cajun English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cajun_English) nor the stereotypical [Southern accent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_American_English) that is often misportrayed by film and television actors. Like earlier Southern Englishes, it features frequent [deletion of the pre-consonantal "r"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rhotic_and_non-rhotic_accents), though the local white dialect also came to be quite similar to [New York accents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_accent).[^cite_note-241] No consensus describes how this happened, but it likely resulted from New Orleans's geographic isolation by water and the fact that the city was a major immigration port throughout the 19th century and early 20th century. Specifically, many members of European immigrant families originally raised in the cities of the Northeast, namely New York, moved to New Orleans during this time frame, bringing their Northeastern accents along with their [Irish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irish_American), [Italian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_American) (especially [Sicilian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sicilian_American)), [German](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_American), and [Jewish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_American) culture.[^cite_note-242]

One of the strongest varieties of the New Orleans accent is sometimes identified as the [Yat dialect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yat_dialect), from the greeting "Where y'at?" This distinctive accent is dying out in the city, but remains strong in the surrounding parishes.

Less visibly, various ethnic groups throughout the area have retained distinct language traditions. The French-speaking community has had a cultural center, the [Alliance Française of New Orleans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alliance_Fran%C3%A7aise_of_New_Orleans), since 1984.[^cite_note-243] The association is a chapter of the international [Alliance Française](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alliance_Fran%C3%A7aise) organization and promotes French language and culture in New Orleans and the surrounding region. Since Louisiana became the first U.S. state to join the *[Organisation Internationale de la Francophonie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organisation_Internationale_de_la_Francophonie)* in 2018, New Orleans has reemerged as an important center for the state's francophone and creolophone cultures and languages, as seen in new organizations such as the Nous Foundation.[^cite_note-244] Although rare, [Louisiana French](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louisiana_French) and [Louisiana Creole](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louisiana_Creole) are still spoken in the city. There is also Louisiana-Canarian Spanish dialect, the [Isleño Spanish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isle%C3%B1o_Spanish), spoken by the [Isleño](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isle%C3%B1o) people and older members of the population.

The [fleur-de-lis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fleur-de-lis) is often a symbol of New Orleans and its sports teams.

New Orleans's professional sports teams include the 2009 [Super Bowl XLIV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_XLIV) champion [New Orleans Saints](21720.md) ([NFL](21211.md)) and the [New Orleans Pelicans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Orleans_Pelicans) ([NBA](22093.md)).[^cite_note-245][^cite_note-246][^cite_note-247] It is also home to the [Big Easy Rollergirls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Easy_Rollergirls), an all-female [flat track roller derby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Women's_Flat_Track_Derby_Association) team, and the [New Orleans Blaze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Orleans_Blaze), a [women's football](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Women's_Football_Association) team.[^cite_note-BlazeFootball-248][^cite_note-BERG-249] New Orleans is also home to two [NCAA Division I](669931.md) athletic programs, the [Tulane Green Wave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tulane_Green_Wave) of the [American Athletic Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Athletic_Conference) and the [UNO Privateers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNO_Privateers) of the [Southland Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southland_Conference).

The [Caesars Superdome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caesars_Superdome) is the home of the Saints, the Sugar Bowl, and other prominent events. It has hosted the [Super Bowl](27718.md) a record eight times ([1978](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_XII), [1981](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_XV), [1986](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_XX), [1990](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_XXIV), [1997](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_XXXI), [2002](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_XXXVI), [2013](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_XLVII), and [2025](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_LIX)). The [Smoothie King Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smoothie_King_Center) is the home of the Pelicans, VooDoo, and many events that are not large enough to need the Superdome. New Orleans is also home to the [Fair Grounds Race Course](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fair_Grounds_Race_Course), the nation's third-oldest thoroughbred track. The city's [Lakefront Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lakefront_Arena) has also been home to sporting events.

Each year New Orleans plays host to the [Sugar Bowl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sugar_Bowl), the [New Orleans Bowl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Orleans_Bowl), the [Bayou Classic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bayou_Classic), and the [Zurich Classic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zurich_Classic_of_New_Orleans), a golf tournament on the [PGA Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PGA_Tour). In addition, it has often hosted major sporting events that have no permanent home, such as the Super Bowl, [ArenaBowl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ArenaBowl), [NBA All-Star Game](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBA_All-Star_Game), [BCS National Championship Game](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BCS_National_Championship_Game), and the [NCAA Final Four](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NCAA_Men's_Division_I_Basketball_Championship). The [Rock 'n' Roll Mardi Gras Marathon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rock_'n'_Roll_Mardi_Gras_Marathon) and the [Crescent City Classic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crescent_City_Classic) are two annual [road running](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Road_running) events.

In 2017, [Major League Rugby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Major_League_Rugby) had its inaugural season, and [NOLA Gold](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Orleans_Gold) were one of the first teams in the league.[^cite_note-250] They play at the [Gold Mine on Airline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gold_Mine_on_Airline), a former [minor league baseball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minor_League_Baseball) stadium in the suburb of [Metairie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metairie,_Louisiana). In 2022, a consortium started an attempt to bring professional [soccer](10568.md) to New Orleans, hoping to place teams in the male [USL Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USL_Championship) and women's [USL Super League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USL_Super_League) by 2025.[^cite_note-251]
